  "toolUseResult":{
    "type":"text",
    "file":{
      "startLine":1,
      "totalLines":144,
      "filePath":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech/issues/322-box-delete-via-runtime-spec-api.md",
      "content":"# 322 — Box delete: the user-facing experience\n\n## Status\nopen · the underlying mechanism is part of 320; this issue\ncovers the user-facing API and the explicit-vs-implicit-deletion\ndesign decision\n\n## Current behavior\n\nOnce issue 320 lands, the runtime has a unified\n`runtime_apply_box_spec(target_id, spec_json)` entry point that\nhandles create / reconfigure / delete based on the spec's\ncontent. The delete path is: install NULL at the graph slot\nvia the same supersession mechanism, let the refcount drive\nthe old box's eventual free.\n\nWhat 320 does NOT cover, and what this issue is for:\n\n- The user-facing spec shape that triggers deletion.\n- A convenience wrapper for the common case.\n- Validation that catches \"I forgot to fill in fields\"\n  accidents from masquerading as deletes.\n- The downstream effects when consumers wired to a deleted box\n  try to push values into the now-gone graph slot.\n\n## Why deletion needs more than just \"any minimum spec\"\n\nThe natural shortcut would be: \"if the spec only has an id and\nno other fields, treat it as a delete.\" Compact and elegant.\n\nBut it's also dangerous: a user who forgets to populate fields\nin their reconfigure spec accidentally deletes the box. Defaults\nshould fail-closed (do nothing surprising) rather than fail-open\n(do something destructive). The minimum-spec-means-delete shape\nfails open.\n\nThe safer alternative is an explicit marker in the spec:\n\n```json\n{\"action\": \"delete\", \"id\": \"target_box_id\"}\n```\n\nThe runtime parses the spec, sees `action == \"delete\"`, and\nroutes through the delete path. Specs without an explicit\n`action` field default to create-or-reconfigure (the inferred\naction based on whether target_id exists). Explicit beats\nimplicit; destructive operations are always explicit.\n\n## Convenience wrapper\n\nFor the common case where a user just wants to delete a box\nthey know the id of, add a sugar function:\n\n```c\nint runtime_destroy_box(const char *target_id, char **err_out);\n```\n\nInternally, this constructs the delete spec JSON and calls\n`runtime_apply_box_spec`. Same code path, friendlier signature.\n\nSame shape per language bridge:\n\n- Lua: `soramech.destroy_box(target_id)`\n- C:   `soramech_destroy_box(target_id, err_buf, err_cap)`\n- Bash: `soramech_destroy_box \"$target_id\"`\n\n## Downstream effects of deletion\n\nA box X is deleted. Some other box Y has a connection pointing\nat X. Y fires, produces an output, attempts to push to X's\ninput slots — but X is gone from the graph slot.\n\nTwo possible behaviours:\n\n- **Silent drop:** the push fails because the target lookup\n  returns NULL. Producer continues; output is lost. A push\n  event with `result=target-gone` records the loss in the\n  JSONL transcript.\n- **Hard error:** the push fails and the producer's fire is\n  treated as a failure. The run aborts (or the producer's\n  spec returns an error code).\n\nThe user's stated preference throughout the project is \"errors\nover fallbacks.\" So hard error is the strict reading. But\ndeletion is a deliberate user action — if they deleted X, they\nprobably know Y depended on it. Hard-error-on-every-push would\nmean a single deleted leaf-box could crash the entire run.\n\nA middle path: silent drop by default, with the JSONL event\nmaking the loss observable, AND a runtime knob (env var or\nspec field) to upgrade the drop to a hard error for users who\nwant strict behaviour.\n\n## Suggested implementation steps\n\n1. **Define the spec shape for delete.** `{\"action\": \"delete\",\n   \"id\": \"...\"}`. Document that the `action` field defaults to\n   \"apply\" (which means create-or-reconfigure based on target\n   existence) and the only other recognised value is \"delete\".\n2. **Implement the delete path inside\n   `runtime_apply_box_spec`.** Already part of 320's\n   implementation; this is just adding the action dispatch.\n3. **Add `runtime_destroy_box` as the sugar wrapper.** Three\n   language bridges plus the C entry point.\n4. **Add the push-target-gone JSONL event.** Same shape as\n   other push events; `result=target-gone` is the new value.\n5. **Add an opt-in strict mode** for users who want push-to-\n   deleted-box to hard-error rather than silent-drop. Env var\n   `SORAMECH_STRICT_DELETE=1` or per-call flag, TBD.\n6. **Fixtures:**\n   - `tests/maps/322-delete-then-orphaned-push` — A box X is\n     created; B is wired to X; X is deleted; B fires once more;\n     the push fails with `result=target-gone` in the JSONL.\n   - `tests/maps/322-delete-and-recreate` — A box X is created,\n     deleted, then created again with the same id and different\n     behaviour. The recreate succeeds at the now-NULL graph\n     slot; subsequent fires use the new behaviour.\n\n## Relevant files\n\n- `src/018-runtime-builtins.{c,h}` — `runtime_destroy_box`\n  sugar wrapper.\n- `src/010-graph-loader.{c,h}` — `parse_box_file` extended to\n  recognise the `action` field on the spec.\n- `src/013-jsonl-events.{c,h}` / `src/014-event-queue.{c,h}` —\n  `target-gone` as a new push result.\n- `langs/lua/spec.c`, `langs/c/spec.c`, `langs/bash/spec.c` —\n  one new bridge function per language for `destroy_box`.\n\n## Open questions\n\n- **Strict-mode default.** Should `SORAMECH_STRICT_DELETE=0`\n  (silent drop default) or `=1` (hard error default) be the\n  shipped default? Silent drop matches \"don't crash the run on\n  a leaf-box deletion\"; hard error matches \"errors over\n  fallbacks.\" Lean toward silent-drop-by-default + JSONL event\n  + opt-in strict; revisit if a project disagrees.\n- **Implicit recreation.** A delete followed by a create at\n  the same id — should this work seamlessly, or require an\n  explicit timing barrier? My instinct: works seamlessly. The\n  graph slot transitions NULL → new-box-pointer atomically;\n  any worker that arrives at the in-between state (graph slot\n  is NULL) just exits gracefully.\n",
      "numLines":144
    }
  },

Aligned to the unified routing schema\n9\t * from issue 233 — every call box carries a routing.kind, and the\n10\t * legacy `comparand` / `iterator_outputs` fields are gone. The data\n11\t * and file_write box kinds from issue 229 are dispatch-layer\n12\t * primitives — they have no function to invoke.\n13\t *\n14\t * Memory model: every string the graph_t holds (box ids, refs,\n15\t * function names, branch tags, paths, input port names) lives in a\n16\t * single json_arena_t owned by the graph_t. graph_destroy frees the\n17\t * arena, the box / connection / input arrays, and the graph_t in\n18\t * one call.\n19\t *\n20\t * Errors: graph_load returns NULL on failure and sets *err to a\n21\t * malloc'd diagnostic of the form \"<file>:<line>: <message>\". These are language-agnostic box kinds — the same\n56\t     * shape as read/write boxes, no `lang` field. Their `do_*`\n57\t     * implementations live in the dispatch layer and call\n58\t     * runtime_create_box / runtime_connect internally.\n59\t     *\n60\t     * The per-language wrappers (langs/lua/spec.c's\n61\t     * `soramech.create_box`, langs/c/soramech.h's\n62\t     * `soramech_create_box`) remain as backwards-compatible\n63\t     * convenience entry points; the box-kind path is the new idiomatic\n64\t     * way and the only path that works uniformly across every\n65\t     * language (including Bash, which has no per-language wrapper). */\n66\t    BOX_CREATE_BOX,  /* construct a new box from a spec on the input wire  */\n67\t    BOX_CONNECT,     /* attach a wire described by a connection-entry input */\n68\t    /* Issue 248: encapsulated map. A box of this kind names a\n69\t     * sub-map directory via `ref`; at graph load the loader\n70\t     * recursively loads the sub-map's boxes, splices them into\n71\t     * the parent with id prefixes, and rewires the parent's\n72\t     * wires that reach this box to instead reach the sub-map's\n73\t     * externally-marked data / write boxes. After inlining the\n74\t     * box itself becomes inert — it never dispatches. */\n75\t    BOX_MAP,\n76\t} box_kind_t;\n77\t/* }}} */\n78\t\n79\t/* {{{ Routing kinds (issue 233 unified schema) */\n80\ttypedef enum {\n81\t    ROUTING_PLAIN,\n82\t    ROUTING_COMPARATOR,\n83\t    ROUTING_ITERATOR,\n84\t    ROUTING_RANDOMIZER,\n85\t    ROUTING_WEIGHTED,\n86\t    ROUTING_DISTRIBUTOR,\n87\t    ROUTING_NONLINEARITY,    /* issue 250 — value-transforming routing */\n88\t} routing_kind_t;\n89\t\n90\t/* Issue 253 — nonlinearity refactor. Normalised at runtime. Port naming follows the band shape:\n115\t     *   value <  t0          → \"below_<t0>\"\n116\t     *   value == t0 == t1    → \"==_<t0>\"        (doubled threshold)\n117\t     *   t_i < value < t_{i+1}→ \"between_<t_i>_<t_{i+1}>\"\n118\t     *   value > t_{n-1}      → \"above_<t_{n-1}>\". */\n119\t    int            n_thresholds;\n120\t    const double  *thresholds;\n121\t    /* Issue 253 — nonlinearity refactor. The mutex pointer is\n131\t     * heap-allocated (so the routing_t struct stays POD-shaped\n132\t     * for the calloc/free pattern the rest of the loader uses);\n133\t     * NULL when the routing kind isn't nonlinearity. */\n134\t    nonlinearity_range_t nl_range;\n135\t    int            nl_memory;            /* ring buffer size N */\n136\t    double         nl_steepness;          /* k; default 1.0 */\n137\t    double        *nl_buffer;             /* N-cell ring; NULL if not nonlinearity */\n138\t    int            nl_write_idx;          /* next slot to overwrite */\n139\t    int            nl_n_filled;           /* how many slots have real data so far */\n140\t    void          *nl_mutex;              /* pthread_mutex_t * */\n141\t} routing_t;\n142\t/* }}} */\n143\t\n144\t/* {{{ Input port declaration */\n145\ttypedef struct {\n146\t    const char *name;       /* port name; arena-owned     */\n147\t    const char *type;       /* \"string\" by convention     */\n148\t    const char *literal;    /* NULL or arena-owned value  */\n149\t    int         optional;   /* issue 230 optional flag    */\n150\t    /* Per-port custom translation shim (issue 246). When non-NULL,\n151\t     * names a file relative to the map dir that contains a\n152\t     * user-written shim. The shim's\n155\t     * language is implied by the box's `lang` field; the file is\n156\t     * compiled / loaded by the appropriate spec's machinery. */\n157\t    const char *custom_translation;\n158\t} input_decl_t;\n159\t/* }}} */\n160\t\n161\t/* {{{ Outgoing connection */\n162\t/* String refs are preserved for diagnostics; resolved integer\n163\t * indices are filled in by the topology pass and let the dispatch\n164\t * layer (issue 304) avoid string lookups at runtime. */\n165\ttypedef struct {\n166\t    const char *to_box;       /* destination box id (arena-owned)        */\n167\t    const char *to_input;     /* destination port name                   */\n168\t    const char *from_branch;  /* NULL for plain output; else branch name */\n169\t    int         to_box_idx;   /* resolved index into graph->boxes        */\n170\t    int         to_input_idx; /* resolved index into target's inputs[]   */\n171\t} connection_t;\n172\t/* }}} */\n173\t\n174\t/* {{{ External binding (issue 248)\n175\t *\n176\t * A `data` box marked externally-supplied OR a `write` box marked\n177\t * externally-consumed carries one of these. The kind picks how the\n178\t * box's port maps to the encapsulating BOX_MAP's input/output\n179\t * port list: `positional` by argument order, `numbered` by an\n180\t * explicit index, `named` by a string. */\n181\ttypedef enum {\n182\t    EXTERNAL_NONE = 0,\n183\t    EXTERNAL_POSITIONAL,\n184\t    EXTERNAL_NUMBERED,\n185\t    EXTERNAL_NAMED,\n186\t} external_kind_t;\n187\t\n188\ttypedef struct {\n189\t    external_kind_t kind;\n190\t    int             index;     /* positional / numbered */\n191\t    const char     *name;      /* named; arena-owned */\n192\t} external_binding_t;\n193\t/* }}} */\n194\t\n195\t/* {{{ Box */\n196\t/* Tagged so the forward declaration `typedef struct box box_t;` in\n197\t * langs/lang-spec.h matches and the compile callback can accept a\n198\t * `const box_t *` parameter. */\n199\ttypedef struct box {\n200\t    const char    *id;\n201\t    box_kind_t     kind;\n202\t\n203\t    /* Call boxes */\n204\t    const char    *lang;          /* \"lua\" / \"c\" / \"bash\" / NULL    */\n205\t    const char    *ref;           /* source file (call only)        */\n206\t    const char    *fn;            /* function name (call only)      */\n207\t    const char    *returns;       /* declared output type: \"string\"/\"int\"/\n208\t                                   * \"double\"/\"bool\"/\"bytes\"/\"json\"/\"void\"\n209\t                                   * or NULL when the user hasn't said.\n210\t                                   * Read by specs on the JSON-output\n211\t                                   * path (issue 307 item 2). */\n212\t    routing_t      routing;       /* call only                      */\n213\t    int            output_capacity; /* 0 means variable-size        */\n214\t\n215\t    /* Per-box compile hints, optional, currently used by the C spec\n216\t     * (issue 307). Lua / Bash boxes leave these at NULL / 0. All\n217\t     * point into the graph's arena; no separate ownership. */\n218\t    const char    *cflags;        /* extra flags appended to `cc`   */\n219\t    int            n_link_libs;\n220\t    const char   **link_libs;     /* each becomes `-l<name>`        */\n221\t    int            n_headers;\n222\t    const char   **headers;       /* `#include`d by generated wrappers */\n223\t\n224\t    /* Read boxes */\n225\t    const char    *path;          /* file path (read only; ignored if value set) */\n226\t    const char    *value;         /* inline literal; if set, read emits this directly */\n227\t\n228\t    /* Issue 248 — externally-supplied / externally-consumed binding.\n229\t     * Present on `data` boxes inside a sub-map that should source\n230\t     * their value from the parent's encapsulating box's input, OR\n231\t     * on `write` boxes whose `value` should also surface back to\n232\t     * the parent. EXTERNAL_NONE means \"not bound to the parent.\"\n233\t     * The loader's encapsulation pass reads this to rewire wires;\n234\t     * the dispatch never looks at it (the rewiring happens\n235\t     * entirely at load time). */\n236\t    external_binding_t external;\n237\t\n238\t    /* Read-box value cache (issue 244): the bytes a downstream consumer\n239\t     * pulls when it needs this read box's value. Populated at graph\n240\t     * load — inline `value` is strdup'd; file `path` is read and the\n241\t     * file's bytes are owned here. Always present on BOX_READ; NULL on\n242\t     * call / write boxes. Freed by graph_destroy. */\n243\t    char          *cached_value;\n244\t    int            cached_size;\n245\t\n246\t    /* Common */\n247\t    int            n_inputs;\n248\t    input_decl_t  *inputs;\n249\t\n250\t    /* Issue 248 — declared output ports for encapsulated maps.\n251\t     * Meaningful only on BOX_MAP records (the documented exception\n252\t     * to issue 218's \"single output per box\" rule). Each entry\n253\t     * declares one output port; the parent wires its own outgoing\n254\t     * connections from the encap box with `from_branch` set to the\n255\t     * port's name. The encapsulation pass matches each such\n256\t     * connection to an externally-consumed write box inside the\n257\t     * sub-map by name (named binding) or by port index\n258\t     * (positional / numbered) and splices the wire onto that write\n259\t     * box's connections array. After the splice the BOX_MAP record\n260\t     * is inert. Other box kinds leave these at 0 / NULL. */\n261\t    int            n_outputs;\n262\t    input_decl_t  *outputs;\n263\t    /* Connections grow at runtime via runtime_connect (issue 319d).\n264\t     * Writers allocate a new array, copy + append, atomic-store the\n265\t     * new connections pointer, then atomic-store the new count;\n266\t     * old arrays are parked on the graph's stale list and freed at\n267\t     * graph_destroy. Readers see either (old, old) / (new, old) /\n268\t     * (new, new) — never (old, new) — so iteration is always safe\n269\t     * against a concurrent grow. Implicit atomic loads via C11\n270\t     * cover the common access patterns. */\n271\t    _Atomic int                n_connections;\n272\t    _Atomic(connection_t *)    connections;\n273\t\n274\t    /* Runtime state — populated by graph_attach_runtime, left at\n275\t     * defaults (-1 / NULL / 0) by graph_load alone. */\n276\t    int            spec_idx;       /* index into the spec registry; -1 if no spec  */\n277\t    int           *input_slot_ids; /* n_inputs entries; -1 if not allocated yet    */\n278\t    int           *input_slot_modes;/* 0 = PEEK (1-cell), 1 = POP (N-cell)         */\n279\t    int            counter_slot_id;/* atomic-counter slot for iterator routing; -1 */\n280\t    int            multi_spawn;    /* 1 if the box may fire many times in one run  */\n281\t\n282\t    /* 312 per-edge fast-path classification.\n283\t     *\n284\t     * One bit per input port: true iff the producer feeding that\n285\t     * port shares this box's language. Drives the consumer's\n286\t     * decoder choice on single-ring slots and seeds the\n287\t     * `input_native[i]` flag that the spec's invoke consults.\n288\t     *\n289\t     * One bit per outgoing connection (not per output port — a\n290\t     * single output may fan to consumers of different languages):\n291\t     * true iff the consumer on the other end of that connection\n292\t     * shares this box's language. When set, the producer pushes\n293\t     * native bytes to the consumer slot's `ring_native`; when\n294\t     * cleared, the producer calls `native_to_json` first and\n295\t     * pushes JSON to `ring_json`. */\n296\t    int           *input_edge_native;   /* n_inputs entries, or NULL              */\n297\t    int           *output_edge_native;  /* n_connections entries, or NULL         */\n298\t\n299\t    /* 244 per-port read-box predecessor lists.\n300\t     *\n301\t     * A consumer's input port may be fed by one or more BOX_READ\n302\t     * value sources. Under the pull-on-demand model these don't push\n303\t     * at startup; instead, when the consumer fires and finds the\n304\t     * slot empty, the dispatch layer pulls the next read box's\n305\t     * cached value into the input buffer directly. With more than\n306\t     * one predecessor on the same port the dispatch rotates via the\n307\t     * counter slot (atomic increment, mod count).\n308\t     *\n309\t     * Allocated by graph_load: n_inputs entries each; entries with\n310\t     * zero read predecessors hold NULL / 0 / -1. Freed by\n311\t     * graph_destroy. */\n312\t    int           *n_read_predecessors;    /* n_inputs entries     */\n313\t    int          **read_predecessor_ids;   /* per-port box-index lists */\n314\t    int           *read_pred_counter_slot; /* per-port; -1 unless >1 preds */\n315\t} box_t;\n316\t\n317\t/* Slot mode constants used by box_t.input_slot_modes[]. */\n318\t#define SLOT_MODE_PEEK 0\n319\t#define SLOT_MODE_POP  1\n320\t/* }}} */\n321\t\n322\t/* {{{ Graph */\n323\ttypedef struct graph graph_t;\n324\t\n325\t/* Read a map directory and build the graph. Returns NULL on any\n326\t * failure and sets *err to a malloc'd \"<file>:<line>: <message>\"\n327\t * diagnostic. The caller frees *err. */\n328\tgraph_t *graph_load(const char *map_dir, char **err);\n329\t\n330\t/* Free the graph and every byte it owns. Safe on NULL. */\n331\tvoid     graph_destroy(graph_t *g);\n332\t\n333\t/* Accessors. */\n334\tconst char  *graph_name        (const graph_t *g);\n335\tconst char  *graph_description (const graph_t *g);\n336\tconst char  *graph_entry_box_id(const graph_t *g);\n337\tconst char  *graph_map_dir     (const graph_t *g);\n338\tint          graph_n_boxes     (const graph_t *g);\n339\tconst box_t *graph_box         (const graph_t *g, int i);\n340\tconst box_t *graph_box_by_id   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);\n341\t\n342\t/* Find a box's index by id; -1 if missing. */\n343\tint          graph_box_index   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);\n344\t\n345\t/* Distinct language names used by call boxes in the map (filled\n346\t * in by graph_attach_runtime). The pool's spec init can use this\n347\t * to skip workers for specs the map doesn't actually use. */\n348\tint          graph_n_languages (const graph_t *g);\n349\tconst char  *graph_language    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n350\t\n351\t/* Entry-box set: indices into graph->boxes of boxes the pool runner\n352\t * submits as initial tasks. A box qualifies when it is BOX_CALL or\n353\t * BOX_WRITE AND every non-optional input is fed only by BOX_READ\n354\t * value sources (or has zero inputs). Filled in by graph_load. */\n355\tint          graph_n_entry_boxes(const graph_t *g);\n356\tint          graph_entry_box    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n357\t\n358\t/* Distinct input-slot cell sizes the graph uses. Filled in by\n359\t * graph_attach_runtime after the per-port slot widths are picked.\n360\t * The slot store can use this to pre-populate free lists. */\n361\tint          graph_n_size_classes(const graph_t *g);\n362\tint          graph_size_class    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n363\t/* }}} */\n364\t\n365\t/* {{{ Runtime mutation primitives (issue 319d)\n366\t *\n367\t * graph_add_box appends a pre-built box record to the graph's\n368\t * index. The caller owns the box record's construction — fills in\n369\t * id / kind / lang / ref / fn / inputs / etc. — and hands the\n370\t * malloc'd `box` pointer to this function. After the call the box\n371\t * is owned by the graph; graph_destroy will free it. Returns the\n372\t * new box's index (>= 0) or -1 on allocation failure.\n373\t *\n374\t * box_add_connection appends a connection entry to the given box's\n375\t * `connections` array via atomic copy-and-publish: a new array is\n376\t * allocated at current_n+1 entries, the old entries are copied in,\n377\t * the new entry is appended, and the new array pointer is published\n378\t * before the new count. The old array is parked on the graph's\n379\t * stale-list and freed at graph_destroy. Concurrent dispatch reads\n380\t * of the producer's connections see either the pre-append snapshot\n381\t * or the post-append snapshot, never a half-published state.\n382\t *\n383\t * Single-spawn box invariant: the dispatch never reads a producer's\n384\t * connections concurrently from two workers (single-spawn CAS guard\n385\t * in dispatch). The common case for box_add_connection is a spec\n386\t * calling `connect()` from inside its own invoke; the producer\n387\t * isn't firing on any other worker at that moment.\n388\t *\n389\t * Returns 0 on success, -1 on allocation failure. */\n390\tint graph_add_box       (graph_t *g, box_t *box);\n391\tint box_add_connection  (graph_t *g, box_t *b, connection_t conn);\n392\t/* }}} */\n393\t\n394\t/* {{{ Runtime attach\n395\t *\n396\t * Allocates one input slot per input port for every box and resolves\n397\t * each call box's language spec from the registry. After this call,\n398\t * the dispatch layer can read inputs via `box->input_slot_ids[i]` and\n399\t * invoke the spec via `spec_registry_at(r, box->spec_idx)`.\n400\t *\n401\t * Per-port slot sizing: cell_capacity = max(declared output_capacity\n402\t * across feeders, default_cell_bytes). The LARGE_VALUE flag is set if\n403\t * any feeder has output_capacity == 0 (variable-size). Multi-spawn\n404\t * boxes (any box reachable from an iterator-routing call box) get an\n405\t * N-cell pop ring; single-spawn boxes get a 1-cell peek slot.\n406\t *\n407\t * Returns 0 on success, -1 with *err set on the first resolution or\n408\t * allocation failure. */\n409\tstruct slot_store;        /* opaque forward decl */\n410\tstruct spec_registry;     /* opaque forward decl */\n411\tint graph_attach_runtime(graph_t *g,\n412\t                         struct slot_store    *slots,\n413\t                         struct spec_registry *specs,\n414\t                         int                   default_cell_bytes,\n415\t                         char                **err);\n416\t/* }}} */\n417\t\n418\t#ifdef __cplusplus\n419\t}\n420\t#endif\n421\t\n422\t#endif /* SORAMECH_GRAPH_LOADER_H */\n423\t"

      "content":"/* src/010-graph-loader.h — C graph loader (phase 3), public API.\n *\n * What it is, in a sentence: reads a map directory off disk, parses\n * every JSON file in it, and produces an in-memory graph_t that the\n * dispatch layer (issue 304) and the pool runner (issue 301) walk\n * to drive a run.\n *\n * Designed in issue 305. The data\n * and file_write box kinds from issue 229 are dispatch-layer\n * primitives — they have no function to invoke.\n *\n * Memory model: every string the graph_t holds (box ids, refs,\n * function names, branch tags, paths, input port names) lives in a\n * single json_arena_t owned by the graph_t. graph_destroy frees the\n * arena, the box / connection / input arrays, and the graph_t in\n * one call.\n *\n * Errors: graph_load returns NULL on failure and sets *err to a\n * malloc'd diagnostic of the form \"<file>:<line>: <message>\". The\n * caller frees *err. The hard-crash policy of the rest of phase 3\n * (issue 303) applies upstream — graph_load's caller decides\n * whether to abort or surface the error.\n *\n * Pipeline: directory walk, JSON parse, per-box schema validation,\n * topology resolution (string refs → integer indices), non-iterator\n * cycle rejection, per-edge same-language classification (issue 312),\n * entry-box detection. graph_attach_runtime then allocates input\n * slots sized per the feeders' declared output_capacity, resolves\n * each call box's language spec, and enumerates the distinct\n * languages and slot size classes the map uses.\n *\n * Errors out of any phase abort the load and return NULL with a\n * diagnostic via *err.\n */\n\n#ifndef SORAMECH_GRAPH_LOADER_H\n#define SORAMECH_GRAPH_LOADER_H\n\n#include <stddef.h>\n#include <stdint.h>\n#include <stdatomic.h>\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\nextern \"C\" {\n#endif\n\n/* {{{ Box kinds */\ntypedef enum {\n    BOX_CALL,        /* runs a function via a language spec                */\n    BOX_READ,        /* value source — inline literal or file at `path`    */\n    BOX_WRITE,       /* file sink — writes one input, emits \"true\" downstream */\n    /* Runtime self-construction (issue 319, design-correction follow-on\n     * to 319d/319e). These are language-agnostic box kinds — the same\n     * shape as read/write boxes, no `lang` field. Their `do_*`\n     * implementations live in the dispatch layer and call\n     * runtime_create_box / runtime_connect internally.\n     *\n     * The per-language wrappers (langs/lua/spec.c's\n     * `soramech.create_box`, langs/c/soramech.h's\n     * `soramech_create_box`) remain as backwards-compatible\n     * convenience entry points; the box-kind path is the new idiomatic\n     * way and the only path that works uniformly across every\n     * language (including Bash, which has no per-language wrapper). */\n    BOX_CREATE_BOX,  /* construct a new box from a spec on the input wire  */\n    BOX_CONNECT,     /* attach a wire described by a connection-entry input */\n    /* Issue 248: encapsulated map. A box of this kind names a\n     * sub-map directory via `ref`; at graph load the loader\n     * recursively loads the sub-map's boxes, splices them into\n     * the parent with id prefixes, and rewires the parent's\n     * wires that reach this box to instead reach the sub-map's\n     * externally-marked data / write boxes. After inlining the\n     * box itself becomes inert — it never dispatches. */\n    BOX_MAP,\n} box_kind_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Routing kinds (issue 233 unified schema) */\ntypedef enum {\n    ROUTING_PLAIN,\n    ROUTING_COMPARATOR,\n    ROUTING_ITERATOR,\n    ROUTING_RANDOMIZER,\n    ROUTING_WEIGHTED,\n    ROUTING_DISTRIBUTOR,\n    ROUTING_NONLINEARITY,    /* issue 250 — value-transforming routing */\n} routing_kind_t;\n\n/* Issue 253 — nonlinearity refactor. The original 250 shipped\n * three intent-named variants; the refactor collapses those to a\n * single `range` toggle (signed picks tanh + [-1, 1]; unit picks\n * sigmoid + [0, 1]). Output is v × score (gated linear unit\n * style), not score alone — soft-AND composition is intrinsic. */\ntypedef enum {\n    NL_SIGNED = 0,            /* default: tanh, output [-1, 1] */\n    NL_UNIT,                  /* sigmoid, output [0, 1] */\n} nonlinearity_range_t;\n\ntypedef struct {\n    routing_kind_t kind;\n    int            n_outputs;   /* iterator / randomizer / weighted / distributor */\n    double         comparand;   /* comparator (single-threshold legacy form) */\n    /* Weighted routing: arena-owned double array of n_outputs\n     * entries. Normalised at runtime. NULL for other kinds. */\n    const double  *weights;\n    /* Issue 243 — multi-band comparator: a non-empty, non-decreasing\n     * array of N thresholds carves the number line into N+1 bands.\n     * Doubled consecutive values (t_i == t_{i+1}) mark zero-width\n     * equality bands, so the legacy single-comparand form is\n     * representable as `thresholds = [c, c]` (the loader fills\n     * thresholds = NULL when only `comparand` is set; dispatch's\n     * comparator branch falls back to the lt/eq/gt path in that\n     * case). Port naming follows the band shape:\n     *   value <  t0          → \"below_<t0>\"\n     *   value == t0 == t1    → \"==_<t0>\"        (doubled threshold)\n     *   t_i < value < t_{i+1}→ \"between_<t_i>_<t_{i+1}>\"\n     *   value > t_{n-1}      → \"above_<t_{n-1}>\". */\n    int            n_thresholds;\n    const double  *thresholds;\n    /* Issue 253 — nonlinearity refactor. The `range` toggle\n     * picks both the output range and the S-curve:\n     *   NL_SIGNED → tanh,    output [-1, 1]\n     *   NL_UNIT   → sigmoid, output [0,  1]\n     * The bounds the input is normalised against come from a\n     * per-box ring buffer holding the last `nl_memory` observed\n     * values. Output is v × score (gated linear unit pattern).\n     *\n     * Concurrency: a per-box pthread_mutex guards the ring\n     * buffer's append + min/max recompute. The mutex pointer is\n     * heap-allocated (so the routing_t struct stays POD-shaped\n     * for the calloc/free pattern the rest of the loader uses);\n     * NULL when the routing kind isn't nonlinearity. */\n    nonlinearity_range_t nl_range;\n    int            nl_memory;            /* ring buffer size N */\n    double         nl_steepness;          /* k; default 1.0 */\n    double        *nl_buffer;             /* N-cell ring; NULL if not nonlinearity */\n    int            nl_write_idx;          /* next slot to overwrite */\n    int            nl_n_filled;           /* how many slots have real data so far */\n    void          *nl_mutex;              /* pthread_mutex_t * */\n} routing_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Input port declaration */\ntypedef struct {\n    const char *name;       /* port name; arena-owned     */\n    const char *type;       /* \"string\" by convention     */\n    const char *literal;    /* NULL or arena-owned value  */\n    int         optional;   /* issue 230 optional flag    */\n    /* Per-port custom translation shim (issue 246). When non-NULL,\n     * names a file relative to the map dir that contains a\n     * user-written shim. The shim runs at the start of the\n     * consumer's task and replaces the default decode for THIS\n     * port only — other ports keep the default path. The shim's\n     * language is implied by the box's `lang` field; the file is\n     * compiled / loaded by the appropriate spec's machinery. */\n    const char *custom_translation;\n} input_decl_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Outgoing connection */\n/* String refs are preserved for diagnostics; resolved integer\n * indices are filled in by the topology pass and let the dispatch\n * layer (issue 304) avoid string lookups at runtime. */\ntypedef struct {\n    const char *to_box;       /* destination box id (arena-owned)        */\n    const char *to_input;     /* destination port name                   */\n    const char *from_branch;  /* NULL for plain output; else branch name */\n    int         to_box_idx;   /* resolved index into graph->boxes        */\n    int         to_input_idx; /* resolved index into target's inputs[]   */\n} connection_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ External binding (issue 248)\n *\n * A `data` box marked externally-supplied OR a `write` box marked\n * externally-consumed carries one of these. The kind picks how the\n * box's port maps to the encapsulating BOX_MAP's input/output\n * port list: `positional` by argument order, `numbered` by an\n * explicit index, `named` by a string. */\ntypedef enum {\n    EXTERNAL_NONE = 0,\n    EXTERNAL_POSITIONAL,\n    EXTERNAL_NUMBERED,\n    EXTERNAL_NAMED,\n} external_kind_t;\n\ntypedef struct {\n    external_kind_t kind;\n    int             index;     /* positional / numbered */\n    const char     *name;      /* named; arena-owned */\n} external_binding_t;\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Box */\n/* Tagged so the forward declaration `typedef struct box box_t;` in\n * langs/lang-spec.h matches and the compile callback can accept a\n * `const box_t *` parameter. */\ntypedef struct box {\n    const char    *id;\n    box_kind_t     kind;\n\n    /* Call boxes */\n    const char    *lang;          /* \"lua\" / \"c\" / \"bash\" / NULL    */\n    const char    *ref;           /* source file (call only)        */\n    const char    *fn;            /* function name (call only)      */\n    const char    *returns;       /* declared output type: \"string\"/\"int\"/\n                                   * \"double\"/\"bool\"/\"bytes\"/\"json\"/\"void\"\n                                   * or NULL when the user hasn't said.\n                                   * Read by specs on the JSON-output\n                                   * path (issue 307 item 2). */\n    routing_t      routing;       /* call only                      */\n    int            output_capacity; /* 0 means variable-size        */\n\n    /* Per-box compile hints, optional, currently used by the C spec\n     * (issue 307). Lua / Bash boxes leave these at NULL / 0. All\n     * point into the graph's arena; no separate ownership. */\n    const char    *cflags;        /* extra flags appended to `cc`   */\n    int            n_link_libs;\n    const char   **link_libs;     /* each becomes `-l<name>`        */\n    int            n_headers;\n    const char   **headers;       /* `#include`d by generated wrappers */\n\n    /* Read boxes */\n    const char    *path;          /* file path (read only; ignored if value set) */\n    const char    *value;         /* inline literal; if set, read emits this directly */\n\n    /* Issue 248 — externally-supplied / externally-consumed binding.\n     * Present on `data` boxes inside a sub-map that should source\n     * their value from the parent's encapsulating box's input, OR\n     * on `write` boxes whose `value` should also surface back to\n     * the parent. EXTERNAL_NONE means \"not bound to the parent.\"\n     * The loader's encapsulation pass reads this to rewire wires;\n     * the dispatch never looks at it (the rewiring happens\n     * entirely at load time). */\n    external_binding_t external;\n\n    /* Read-box value cache (issue 244): the bytes a downstream consumer\n     * pulls when it needs this read box's value. Populated at graph\n     * load — inline `value` is strdup'd; file `path` is read and the\n     * file's bytes are owned here. Always present on BOX_READ; NULL on\n     * call / write boxes. Freed by graph_destroy. */\n    char          *cached_value;\n    int            cached_size;\n\n    /* Common */\n    int            n_inputs;\n    input_decl_t  *inputs;\n\n    /* Issue 248 — declared output ports for encapsulated maps.\n     * Meaningful only on BOX_MAP records (the documented exception\n     * to issue 218's \"single output per box\" rule). Each entry\n     * declares one output port; the parent wires its own outgoing\n     * connections from the encap box with `from_branch` set to the\n     * port's name. The encapsulation pass matches each such\n     * connection to an externally-consumed write box inside the\n     * sub-map by name (named binding) or by port index\n     * (positional / numbered) and splices the wire onto that write\n     * box's connections array. After the splice the BOX_MAP record\n     * is inert. Other box kinds leave these at 0 / NULL. */\n    int            n_outputs;\n    input_decl_t  *outputs;\n    /* Connections grow at runtime via runtime_connect (issue 319d).\n     * Writers allocate a new array, copy + append, atomic-store the\n     * new connections pointer, then atomic-store the new count;\n     * old arrays are parked on the graph's stale list and freed at\n     * graph_destroy. Readers see either (old, old) / (new, old) /\n     * (new, new) — never (old, new) — so iteration is always safe\n     * against a concurrent grow. Implicit atomic loads via C11\n     * cover the common access patterns. */\n    _Atomic int                n_connections;\n    _Atomic(connection_t *)    connections;\n\n    /* Runtime state — populated by graph_attach_runtime, left at\n     * defaults (-1 / NULL / 0) by graph_load alone. */\n    int            spec_idx;       /* index into the spec registry; -1 if no spec  */\n    int           *input_slot_ids; /* n_inputs entries; -1 if not allocated yet    */\n    int           *input_slot_modes;/* 0 = PEEK (1-cell), 1 = POP (N-cell)         */\n    int            counter_slot_id;/* atomic-counter slot for iterator routing; -1 */\n    int            multi_spawn;    /* 1 if the box may fire many times in one run  */\n\n    /* 312 per-edge fast-path classification.\n     *\n     * One bit per input port: true iff the producer feeding that\n     * port shares this box's language. Drives the consumer's\n     * decoder choice on single-ring slots and seeds the\n     * `input_native[i]` flag that the spec's invoke consults.\n     *\n     * One bit per outgoing connection (not per output port — a\n     * single output may fan to consumers of different languages):\n     * true iff the consumer on the other end of that connection\n     * shares this box's language. When set, the producer pushes\n     * native bytes to the consumer slot's `ring_native`; when\n     * cleared, the producer calls `native_to_json` first and\n     * pushes JSON to `ring_json`. */\n    int           *input_edge_native;   /* n_inputs entries, or NULL              */\n    int           *output_edge_native;  /* n_connections entries, or NULL         */\n\n    /* 244 per-port read-box predecessor lists.\n     *\n     * A consumer's input port may be fed by one or more BOX_READ\n     * value sources. Under the pull-on-demand model these don't push\n     * at startup; instead, when the consumer fires and finds the\n     * slot empty, the dispatch layer pulls the next read box's\n     * cached value into the input buffer directly. With more than\n     * one predecessor on the same port the dispatch rotates via the\n     * counter slot (atomic increment, mod count).\n     *\n     * Allocated by graph_load: n_inputs entries each; entries with\n     * zero read predecessors hold NULL / 0 / -1. Freed by\n     * graph_destroy. */\n    int           *n_read_predecessors;    /* n_inputs entries     */\n    int          **read_predecessor_ids;   /* per-port box-index lists */\n    int           *read_pred_counter_slot; /* per-port; -1 unless >1 preds */\n} box_t;\n\n/* Slot mode constants used by box_t.input_slot_modes[]. */\n#define SLOT_MODE_PEEK 0\n#define SLOT_MODE_POP  1\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Graph */\ntypedef struct graph graph_t;\n\n/* Read a map directory and build the graph. Returns NULL on any\n * failure and sets *err to a malloc'd \"<file>:<line>: <message>\"\n * diagnostic. The caller frees *err. */\ngraph_t *graph_load(const char *map_dir, char **err);\n\n/* Free the graph and every byte it owns. Safe on NULL. */\nvoid     graph_destroy(graph_t *g);\n\n/* Accessors. */\nconst char  *graph_name        (const graph_t *g);\nconst char  *graph_description (const graph_t *g);\nconst char  *graph_entry_box_id(const graph_t *g);\nconst char  *graph_map_dir     (const graph_t *g);\nint          graph_n_boxes     (const graph_t *g);\nconst box_t *graph_box         (const graph_t *g, int i);\nconst box_t *graph_box_by_id   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);\n\n/* Find a box's index by id; -1 if missing. */\nint          graph_box_index   (const graph_t *g, const char *id);\n\n/* Distinct language names used by call boxes in the map (filled\n * in by graph_attach_runtime). The pool's spec init can use this\n * to skip workers for specs the map doesn't actually use. */\nint          graph_n_languages (const graph_t *g);\nconst char  *graph_language    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n\n/* Entry-box set: indices into graph->boxes of boxes the pool runner\n * submits as initial tasks. A box qualifies when it is BOX_CALL or\n * BOX_WRITE AND every non-optional input is fed only by BOX_READ\n * value sources (or has zero inputs). Filled in by graph_load. */\nint          graph_n_entry_boxes(const graph_t *g);\nint          graph_entry_box    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n\n/* Distinct input-slot cell sizes the graph uses. Filled in by\n * graph_attach_runtime after the per-port slot widths are picked.\n * The slot store can use this to pre-populate free lists. */\nint          graph_n_size_classes(const graph_t *g);\nint          graph_size_class    (const graph_t *g, int i);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Runtime mutation primitives (issue 319d)\n *\n * graph_add_box appends a pre-built box record to the graph's\n * index. The caller owns the box record's construction — fills in\n * id / kind / lang / ref / fn / inputs / etc. — and hands the\n * malloc'd `box` pointer to this function. After the call the box\n * is owned by the graph; graph_destroy will free it. Returns the\n * new box's index (>= 0) or -1 on allocation failure.\n *\n * box_add_connection appends a connection entry to the given box's\n * `connections` array via atomic copy-and-publish: a new array is\n * allocated at current_n+1 entries, the old entries are copied in,\n * the new entry is appended, and the new array pointer is published\n * before the new count. The old array is parked on the graph's\n * stale-list and freed at graph_destroy. Concurrent dispatch reads\n * of the producer's connections see either the pre-append snapshot\n * or the post-append snapshot, never a half-published state.\n *\n * Single-spawn box invariant: the dispatch never reads a producer's\n * connections concurrently from two workers (single-spawn CAS guard\n * in dispatch). The common case for box_add_connection is a spec\n * calling `connect()` from inside its own invoke; the producer\n * isn't firing on any other worker at that moment.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 on allocation failure. */\nint graph_add_box       (graph_t *g, box_t *box);\nint box_add_connection  (graph_t *g, box_t *b, connection_t conn);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Runtime attach\n *\n * Allocates one input slot per input port for every box and resolves\n * each call box's language spec from the registry. After this call,\n * the dispatch layer can read inputs via `box->input_slot_ids[i]` and\n * invoke the spec via `spec_registry_at(r, box->spec_idx)`.\n *\n * Per-port slot sizing: cell_capacity = max(declared output_capacity\n * across feeders, default_cell_bytes). The LARGE_VALUE flag is set if\n * any feeder has output_capacity == 0 (variable-size). Multi-spawn\n * boxes (any box reachable from an iterator-routing call box) get an\n * N-cell pop ring; single-spawn boxes get a 1-cell peek slot.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 with *err set on the first resolution or\n * allocation failure. */\nstruct slot_store;        /* opaque forward decl */\nstruct spec_registry;     /* opaque forward decl */\nint graph_attach_runtime(graph_t *g,\n                         struct slot_store    *slots,\n                         struct spec_registry *specs,\n                         int                   default_cell_bytes,\n                         char                **err);\n/* }}} */\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\n}\n#endif\n\n#endif /* SORAMECH_GRAPH_LOADER_H */\n",
      "numLines":423
    }
  },

}

```json
{
  "cwd":"/mnt/mtwo/programs/sora/soramech",
  "type":"user",

        "tool_use_id":"toolu_01EEhjkHYU6b7L668mExGf6n",

        "content":"1\t/* src/009-slot-store.h — per-input-port slot store, public API.\n2\t *\n3\t * What it is, in a sentence: a thread-safe pool of ring-buffer\n4\t * slots, allocated at graph load time, used by the phase 3\n5\t * dispatch layer to route values between boxes.\n6\t *\n7\t * Slots belong to input ports, not tasks. Each box has one slot\n8\t * per input port; the slot persists for the whole run. A wire is\n9\t * a routing declaration — when a producer's task finishes, the\n10\t * dispatch layer pushes copies of the output into every\n11\t * downstream input slot. Fan-out is N pushes; fan-in is N\n12\t * producers pushing into one slot. Designed in issue 302.\n13\t *\n14\t * Three slot modes, chosen via flags at slot_alloc:\n15\t *\n16\t *   1-cell peek (n_cells == 1, no flags)\n17\t *     — for literals and wires from producers that run exactly\n18\t *       once. Push once at startup. Every consumer task peeks\n19\t *       without draining; the cell stays filled.\n20\t *\n21\t *   N-cell pop (n_cells > 1, no flags)\n22\t *     — for wires whose producer runs many times (iterators,\n23\t *       queued inputs). Push appends to tail; pop drains head\n24\t *       FIFO. Consumer task pops one cell per spawn.\n25\t *\n26\t *   N-cell pop, tagged (SLOT_TAGGED)\n27\t *     — same shape as N-cell pop, but pop returns the cell with\n28\t *       the lowest tag, not the head. Used downstream of parallel\n29\t *       iterators where push order is non-deterministic but\n30\t *       consumption order must follow the iterator counter.\n31\t *\n32\t *   atomic counter (SLOT_ATOMIC_COUNTER, n_cells and cell_capacity\n33\t *     ignored)\n34\t *     — single atomic uint32. Read-and-increment via\n35\t *       slot_read_inc(). Used for iterator / randomizer /\n36\t *       weighted-routing counters. Concurrent reads each get a\n37\t *       distinct value mod the caller-supplied bound.\n38\t *\n39\t *   large-value (SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE)\n40\t *     — for outputs whose size is not known at compile time\n41\t *       (multi-MB LLM responses, dynamically-sized arrays). The\n42\t *       cell holds only a pointer; the payload bytes live in a\n43\t *       large-value heap owned by the slot store. cell_capacity\n44\t *       is ignored — push/pop accept any size. May be combined\n45\t *       with SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED to get lowest-tag pop semantics\n46\t *       over variable-size values.\n47\t *\n48\t * Synchronization is per-slot. There is no allocator-wide lock on\n49\t * the hot read/write path. The atomic-counter slot uses\n50\t * atomic_fetch_add; the ring-buffer slots use a per-slot spinlock\n51\t * around header mutation (one byte, atomic_flag).\n52\t *\n53\t * Lifetime is the run. Slots are freed when the store is\n54\t * destroyed at the end of the run — there is no per-slot refcount\n55\t * and no end-of-stream propagation. Run termination is governed\n56\t * by the pool's active-task counter (issue 301).\n57\t *\n58\t * Errors: a return of -1 from a read/write op means the operation\n59\t * could not be performed (empty pop, buffer too small, type\n60\t * mismatch). The store does not assert or abort; the dispatch\n61\t * layer is expected to check return codes. The hard-crash policy\n62\t * applies at the language-spec layer, not here.\n63\t *\n64\t * What's NOT in this version:\n65\t *  - Size-class free lists and coalescing. The design in issue 302\n66\t *    describes a segregated allocator with eager-coalesce; this\n67\t *    initial implementation uses plain malloc per slot. The pattern\n68\t *    is \"allocate once at graph load, free at run end\", so free-list\n69\t *    optimization buys nothing for the current usage. Lands as a\n70\t *    follow-on if profiling justifies it.\n71\t *\n72\t * Variable-size payload support: slots with SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE\n73\t * store payload bytes in the unified allocator (src/016-…) and the\n74\t * cell only holds an opaque chunk handle. Push allocates a chunk;\n75\t * pop copies the bytes out and drops the chunk's last reference so\n76\t * the bytes return to the allocator's free-lists. The store\n77\t * lazy-creates its allocator on the first LARGE_VALUE slot_alloc\n78\t * and destroys it with the store.\n79\t *\n80\t * (Issue 302's earlier shape used an append-only arena for this\n81\t * path; that arena leaked by design and was retracted on\n82\t * 2026-05-19 in favor of the reclamation-capable allocator above.)\n83\t */\n84\t\n85\t#ifndef SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H\n86\t#define SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H\n87\t\n88\t#include <stdint.h>\n89\t\n90\t#ifdef __cplusplus\n91\textern \"C\" {\n92\t#endif\n93\t\n94\t/* {{{ Types & flags */\n95\ttypedef struct slot_store slot_store_t;\n96\ttypedef int32_t           slot_id_t;\n97\t\n98\t/* Forward declaration of the unified allocator handle. Callers that\n99\t * want to use it (dispatch, for example, allocating its own value\n100\t * buffers) include the allocator header directly; callers that\n101\t * only push/pop bytes through the slot API don't need to know it\n102\t * exists. */\n103\tstruct ua;\n104\t\n105\t#define SLOT_INVALID  ((slot_id_t)-1)\n106\t\n107\t/* slot_alloc flags */\n108\tenum {\n109\t    SLOT_FLAG_NONE           = 0,\n110\t    SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED         = 1 << 0,\n111\t    SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER = 1 << 1,\n112\t    SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE    = 1 << 2,\n113\t    /* Dual-ring slot (issue 312). The slot internally carries three\n114\t     * rings — a native-bytes ring, a JSON-bytes ring, and an ordering\n115\t     * ring whose cells are (which_ring, idx) tuples in arrival order.\n116\t     * Producers push to native or JSON depending on whether the wire\n117\t     * is same-language or crosses a language boundary; consumers pop\n118\t     * via the ordering ring to preserve arrival order across the two\n119\t     * data rings. Use slot_push_native / slot_push_json / slot_pop_ordered\n120\t     * instead of slot_push / slot_pop. All three rings share one\n121\t     * per-slot lock so a push's data write and ordering write are\n122\t     * atomic together. */\n123\t    SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING      = 1 << 3,\n124\t};\n125\t\n126\t/* which_ring values returned by slot_pop_ordered and accepted by the\n127\t * dual-ring push variants. */\n128\tenum {\n129\t    SLOT_RING_NATIVE = 0,\n130\t    SLOT_RING_JSON   = 1,\n131\t};\n132\t/* }}} */\n133\t\n134\t/* {{{ Store lifecycle */\n135\t/* Construct an empty store. Returns NULL on allocation failure. */\n136\tslot_store_t *slot_store_create(void);\n137\t\n138\t/* Free every slot and the store itself. Safe to call on NULL. */\n139\tvoid          slot_store_destroy(slot_store_t *s);\n140\t/* }}} */\n141\t\n142\t/* {{{ Slot lifecycle */\n143\t/* Allocate a slot.\n144\t *\n145\t *   cell_capacity — bytes per cell. Ignored for SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n146\t *   n_cells       — ring size (1 for peek slots, >1 for pop). Ignored for\n147\t *                   SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n148\t *   flags         — zero, SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED, or SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n149\t *\n150\t * Returns the new slot's id, or SLOT_INVALID on allocation failure or\n151\t * invalid arguments. Ids are stable for the lifetime of the store. */\n152\tslot_id_t     slot_alloc(slot_store_t *s,\n153\t                         int32_t cell_capacity,\n154\t                         int32_t n_cells,\n155\t                         int32_t flags);\n156\t/* }}} */\n157\t\n158\t/* {{{ Ring-buffer ops */\n159\t/* Push a value into the slot's next free cell.\n160\t *\n161\t *   data, size — value bytes. size must be <= cell_capacity.\n162\t *   tag        — ordering tag. Carried only on SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED slots;\n163\t *                ignored otherwise.\n164\t *\n165\t * Returns 0 on success, -1 if the slot is full or arguments invalid.\n166\t * Not valid on SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER slots (returns -1). */\n167\tint           slot_push(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n168\t                        const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n169\t\n170\t/* Peek at the head cell without draining it.\n171\t *\n172\t *   buf, buf_size — destination. The number of bytes copied is min of\n173\t *                   the cell's filled_size and buf_size.\n174\t *\n175\t * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 if the slot is empty,\n176\t * the buffer is too small, or the slot is the wrong type. The cell\n177\t * remains filled and may be re-peeked by subsequent tasks (1-cell peek\n178\t * mode). */\n179\tint32_t       slot_peek(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n180\t                        void *buf, int32_t buf_size);\n181\t\n182\t/* Drain one cell from the slot.\n183\t *\n184\t *   buf, buf_size — destination.\n185\t *\n186\t * For untagged slots, returns the head cell and advances head FIFO.\n187\t * For SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED slots, returns the cell with the lowest tag\n188\t * among the filled cells and marks that cell empty (head advances as\n189\t * front-of-ring cells empty).\n190\t *\n191\t * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 on empty / wrong type\n192\t * / buffer too small. */\n193\tint32_t       slot_pop(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n194\t                       void *buf, int32_t buf_size);\n195\t\n196\t/* Dual-ring push: write `data` into the slot's native ring (issue 312)\n197\t * and append the (NATIVE, idx) tuple to the ordering ring. The data\n198\t * write and ordering write are atomic together — pops see them as a\n199\t * pair or not at all.\n200\t *\n201\t * Only valid on SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING slots. On non-dual slots, aliases\n202\t * to slot_push (the ordering ring isn't consulted; back-compat for\n203\t * callers that want one entry point regardless of slot shape).\n204\t *\n205\t * Returns 0 on success, -1 if the slot is full, arguments are\n206\t * invalid, or the slot is the wrong type (e.g. ATOMIC_COUNTER). */\n207\tint           slot_push_native(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n208\t                               const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n209\t\n210\t/* Dual-ring push: write `data` into the slot's JSON ring and append\n211\t * the (JSON, idx) tuple to the ordering ring. Atomicity is the same\n212\t * as slot_push_native.\n213\t *\n214\t * Only valid on SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING slots. On non-dual slots returns\n215\t * -1 — the caller has to use slot_push for the single-ring case.\n216\t *\n217\t * Returns 0 on success, -1 on full / invalid / wrong type. */\n218\tint           slot_push_json  (slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n219\t                               const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n220\t\n221\t/* Dual-ring pop: read the next entry from the ordering ring,\n222\t * then drain the indicated cell from the matching data ring.\n223\t * `*out_which_ring` receives SLOT_RING_NATIVE or SLOT_RING_JSON.\n224\t *\n225\t * On non-dual slots, behaves like slot_pop and sets\n226\t * `*out_which_ring = SLOT_RING_NATIVE`.\n227\t *\n228\t * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 on empty / wrong\n229\t * type / buffer too small. */\n230\tint32_t       slot_pop_ordered(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n231\t                               void *buf, int32_t buf_size,\n232\t                               int32_t *out_which_ring);\n233\t\n234\t/* Atomic read-and-increment on SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER slots.\n235\t *\n236\t *   mod — modulus for the returned value. Pass UINT32_MAX for \"no\n237\t *         modulus\".\n238\t *\n239\t * Returns the pre-increment counter value mod the supplied bound.\n240\t * Concurrent readers each get a distinct value (the underlying\n241\t * counter is incremented atomically). Returns UINT32_MAX on type\n242\t * mismatch — the dispatch layer is expected to call this op only\n243\t * on slots it allocated with SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER. */\n244\tuint32_t      slot_read_inc(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id, uint32_t mod);\n245\t\n246\t/* Check whether the slot has any filled cell available for read.\n247\t * Used by the dispatch layer's spawn-on-input-ready rule (issue 304).\n248\t *\n249\t * Returns 1 if a value is available, 0 otherwise. For ATOMIC_COUNTER\n250\t * slots, always returns 1 (the counter is always readable). */\n251\tint           slot_has_value(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n252\t/* }}} */\n253\t\n254\t/* {{{ Inspection (test / debug) */\n255\t/* Number of filled cells. For ATOMIC_COUNTER slots, returns 0. */\n256\tint32_t       slot_fill_count(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n257\t\n258\t/* Slot count in the store. */\n259\tint32_t       slot_store_size(slot_store_t *s);\n260\t\n261\t/* Read the flag bits a slot was allocated with. Used by dispatch\n262\t * to branch on `SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING` and pick the right push / pop\n263\t * variant per slot. Returns the flag mask on success, -1 on invalid\n264\t * slot id. */\n265\tint32_t       slot_flags(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n266\t\n267\t/* Bytes currently held by SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE payloads — i.e.\n268\t * the unified allocator's \"in use\" total for this store. Returns 0\n269\t * if the store has no allocator (impossible after the cell-array\n270\t * path moved onto the allocator, but kept defensive).\n271\t *\n272\t * Used by the reclamation regression test (issue 302): a slot that\n273\t * pushes-then-pops many times should observe this number stay\n274\t * bounded, proving the variable-size path no longer leaks. */\n275\tuint64_t      slot_store_large_value_bytes_in_use(slot_store_t *s);\n276\t\n277\t/* The unified allocator that owns this store's slot memory. The\n278\t * dispatch layer uses it for the per-task input and output buffers\n279\t * so those allocations participate in the same refcount-driven\n280\t * recycling as the slot cells and the large-value payloads — one\n281\t * heap for every byte that carries a value through the run. May\n282\t * return NULL only if the store itself is NULL. */\n283\tstruct ua    *slot_store_allocator(slot_store_t *s);\n284\t/* }}} */\n285\t\n286\t#ifdef __cplusplus\n287\t}\n288\t#endif\n289\t\n290\t#endif /* SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H */\n291\t"

      "content":"/* src/009-slot-store.h — per-input-port slot store, public API.\n *\n * What it is, in a sentence: a thread-safe pool of ring-buffer\n * slots, allocated at graph load time, used by the phase 3\n * dispatch layer to route values between boxes.\n *\n * Slots belong to input ports, not tasks. Each box has one slot\n * per input port; the slot persists for the whole run. A wire is\n * a routing declaration — when a producer's task finishes, the\n * dispatch layer pushes copies of the output into every\n * downstream input slot. Fan-out is N pushes; fan-in is N\n * producers pushing into one slot. Designed in issue 302.\n *\n * Three slot modes, chosen via flags at slot_alloc:\n *\n *   1-cell peek (n_cells == 1, no flags)\n *     — for literals and wires from producers that run exactly\n *       once. Push once at startup. Every consumer task peeks\n *       without draining; the cell stays filled.\n *\n *   N-cell pop (n_cells > 1, no flags)\n *     — for wires whose producer runs many times (iterators,\n *       queued inputs). Push appends to tail; pop drains head\n *       FIFO. Consumer task pops one cell per spawn.\n *\n *   N-cell pop, tagged (SLOT_TAGGED)\n *     — same shape as N-cell pop, but pop returns the cell with\n *       the lowest tag, not the head. Used downstream of parallel\n *       iterators where push order is non-deterministic but\n *       consumption order must follow the iterator counter.\n *\n *   atomic counter (SLOT_ATOMIC_COUNTER, n_cells and cell_capacity\n *     ignored)\n *     — single atomic uint32. Read-and-increment via\n *       slot_read_inc(). Used for iterator / randomizer /\n *       weighted-routing counters. Concurrent reads each get a\n *       distinct value mod the caller-supplied bound.\n *\n *   large-value (SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE)\n *     — for outputs whose size is not known at compile time\n *       (multi-MB LLM responses, dynamically-sized arrays). The\n *       cell holds only a pointer; the payload bytes live in a\n *       large-value heap owned by the slot store. cell_capacity\n *       is ignored — push/pop accept any size. May be combined\n *       with SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED to get lowest-tag pop semantics\n *       over variable-size values.\n *\n * Synchronization is per-slot. There is no allocator-wide lock on\n * the hot read/write path. The atomic-counter slot uses\n * atomic_fetch_add; the ring-buffer slots use a per-slot spinlock\n * around header mutation (one byte, atomic_flag).\n *\n * Lifetime is the run. Slots are freed when the store is\n * destroyed at the end of the run — there is no per-slot refcount\n * and no end-of-stream propagation. Run termination is governed\n * by the pool's active-task counter (issue 301).\n *\n * Errors: a return of -1 from a read/write op means the operation\n * could not be performed (empty pop, buffer too small, type\n * mismatch). The store does not assert or abort; the dispatch\n * layer is expected to check return codes. The hard-crash policy\n * applies at the language-spec layer, not here.\n *\n * What's NOT in this version:\n *  - Size-class free lists and coalescing. The design in issue 302\n *    describes a segregated allocator with eager-coalesce; this\n *    initial implementation uses plain malloc per slot. The pattern\n *    is \"allocate once at graph load, free at run end\", so free-list\n *    optimization buys nothing for the current usage. Lands as a\n *    follow-on if profiling justifies it.\n *\n * Variable-size payload support: slots with SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE\n * store payload bytes in the unified allocator (src/016-…) and the\n * cell only holds an opaque chunk handle. Push allocates a chunk;\n * pop copies the bytes out and drops the chunk's last reference so\n * the bytes return to the allocator's free-lists. The store\n * lazy-creates its allocator on the first LARGE_VALUE slot_alloc\n * and destroys it with the store.\n *\n * (Issue 302's earlier shape used an append-only arena for this\n * path; that arena leaked by design and was retracted on\n * 2026-05-19 in favor of the reclamation-capable allocator above.)\n */\n\n#ifndef SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H\n#define SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H\n\n#include <stdint.h>\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\nextern \"C\" {\n#endif\n\n/* {{{ Types & flags */\ntypedef struct slot_store slot_store_t;\ntypedef int32_t           slot_id_t;\n\n/* Forward declaration of the unified allocator handle. Callers that\n * want to use it (dispatch, for example, allocating its own value\n * buffers) include the allocator header directly; callers that\n * only push/pop bytes through the slot API don't need to know it\n * exists. */\nstruct ua;\n\n#define SLOT_INVALID  ((slot_id_t)-1)\n\n/* slot_alloc flags */\nenum {\n    SLOT_FLAG_NONE           = 0,\n    SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED         = 1 << 0,\n    SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER = 1 << 1,\n    SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE    = 1 << 2,\n    /* Dual-ring slot (issue 312). The slot internally carries three\n     * rings — a native-bytes ring, a JSON-bytes ring, and an ordering\n     * ring whose cells are (which_ring, idx) tuples in arrival order.\n     * Producers push to native or JSON depending on whether the wire\n     * is same-language or crosses a language boundary; consumers pop\n     * via the ordering ring to preserve arrival order across the two\n     * data rings. Use slot_push_native / slot_push_json / slot_pop_ordered\n     * instead of slot_push / slot_pop. All three rings share one\n     * per-slot lock so a push's data write and ordering write are\n     * atomic together. */\n    SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING      = 1 << 3,\n};\n\n/* which_ring values returned by slot_pop_ordered and accepted by the\n * dual-ring push variants. */\nenum {\n    SLOT_RING_NATIVE = 0,\n    SLOT_RING_JSON   = 1,\n};\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Store lifecycle */\n/* Construct an empty store. Returns NULL on allocation failure. */\nslot_store_t *slot_store_create(void);\n\n/* Free every slot and the store itself. Safe to call on NULL. */\nvoid          slot_store_destroy(slot_store_t *s);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Slot lifecycle */\n/* Allocate a slot.\n *\n *   cell_capacity — bytes per cell. Ignored for SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n *   n_cells       — ring size (1 for peek slots, >1 for pop). Ignored for\n *                   SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n *   flags         — zero, SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED, or SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER.\n *\n * Returns the new slot's id, or SLOT_INVALID on allocation failure or\n * invalid arguments. Ids are stable for the lifetime of the store. */\nslot_id_t     slot_alloc(slot_store_t *s,\n                         int32_t cell_capacity,\n                         int32_t n_cells,\n                         int32_t flags);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Ring-buffer ops */\n/* Push a value into the slot's next free cell.\n *\n *   data, size — value bytes. size must be <= cell_capacity.\n *   tag        — ordering tag. Carried only on SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED slots;\n *                ignored otherwise.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 if the slot is full or arguments invalid.\n * Not valid on SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER slots (returns -1). */\nint           slot_push(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                        const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n\n/* Peek at the head cell without draining it.\n *\n *   buf, buf_size — destination. The number of bytes copied is min of\n *                   the cell's filled_size and buf_size.\n *\n * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 if the slot is empty,\n * the buffer is too small, or the slot is the wrong type. The cell\n * remains filled and may be re-peeked by subsequent tasks (1-cell peek\n * mode). */\nint32_t       slot_peek(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                        void *buf, int32_t buf_size);\n\n/* Drain one cell from the slot.\n *\n *   buf, buf_size — destination.\n *\n * For untagged slots, returns the head cell and advances head FIFO.\n * For SLOT_FLAG_TAGGED slots, returns the cell with the lowest tag\n * among the filled cells and marks that cell empty (head advances as\n * front-of-ring cells empty).\n *\n * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 on empty / wrong type\n * / buffer too small. */\nint32_t       slot_pop(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                       void *buf, int32_t buf_size);\n\n/* Dual-ring push: write `data` into the slot's native ring (issue 312)\n * and append the (NATIVE, idx) tuple to the ordering ring. The data\n * write and ordering write are atomic together — pops see them as a\n * pair or not at all.\n *\n * Only valid on SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING slots. On non-dual slots, aliases\n * to slot_push (the ordering ring isn't consulted; back-compat for\n * callers that want one entry point regardless of slot shape).\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 if the slot is full, arguments are\n * invalid, or the slot is the wrong type (e.g. ATOMIC_COUNTER). */\nint           slot_push_native(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                               const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n\n/* Dual-ring push: write `data` into the slot's JSON ring and append\n * the (JSON, idx) tuple to the ordering ring. Atomicity is the same\n * as slot_push_native.\n *\n * Only valid on SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING slots. On non-dual slots returns\n * -1 — the caller has to use slot_push for the single-ring case.\n *\n * Returns 0 on success, -1 on full / invalid / wrong type. */\nint           slot_push_json  (slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                               const void *data, int32_t size, uint32_t tag);\n\n/* Dual-ring pop: read the next entry from the ordering ring,\n * then drain the indicated cell from the matching data ring.\n * `*out_which_ring` receives SLOT_RING_NATIVE or SLOT_RING_JSON.\n *\n * On non-dual slots, behaves like slot_pop and sets\n * `*out_which_ring = SLOT_RING_NATIVE`.\n *\n * Returns the cell's filled_size on success, -1 on empty / wrong\n * type / buffer too small. */\nint32_t       slot_pop_ordered(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id,\n                               void *buf, int32_t buf_size,\n                               int32_t *out_which_ring);\n\n/* Atomic read-and-increment on SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER slots.\n *\n *   mod — modulus for the returned value. Pass UINT32_MAX for \"no\n *         modulus\".\n *\n * Returns the pre-increment counter value mod the supplied bound.\n * Concurrent readers each get a distinct value (the underlying\n * counter is incremented atomically). Returns UINT32_MAX on type\n * mismatch — the dispatch layer is expected to call this op only\n * on slots it allocated with SLOT_FLAG_ATOMIC_COUNTER. */\nuint32_t      slot_read_inc(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id, uint32_t mod);\n\n/* Check whether the slot has any filled cell available for read.\n * Used by the dispatch layer's spawn-on-input-ready rule (issue 304).\n *\n * Returns 1 if a value is available, 0 otherwise. For ATOMIC_COUNTER\n * slots, always returns 1 (the counter is always readable). */\nint           slot_has_value(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n/* }}} */\n\n/* {{{ Inspection (test / debug) */\n/* Number of filled cells. For ATOMIC_COUNTER slots, returns 0. */\nint32_t       slot_fill_count(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n\n/* Slot count in the store. */\nint32_t       slot_store_size(slot_store_t *s);\n\n/* Read the flag bits a slot was allocated with. Used by dispatch\n * to branch on `SLOT_FLAG_DUAL_RING` and pick the right push / pop\n * variant per slot. Returns the flag mask on success, -1 on invalid\n * slot id. */\nint32_t       slot_flags(slot_store_t *s, slot_id_t id);\n\n/* Bytes currently held by SLOT_FLAG_LARGE_VALUE payloads — i.e.\n * the unified allocator's \"in use\" total for this store. Returns 0\n * if the store has no allocator (impossible after the cell-array\n * path moved onto the allocator, but kept defensive).\n *\n * Used by the reclamation regression test (issue 302): a slot that\n * pushes-then-pops many times should observe this number stay\n * bounded, proving the variable-size path no longer leaks. */\nuint64_t      slot_store_large_value_bytes_in_use(slot_store_t *s);\n\n/* The unified allocator that owns this store's slot memory. The\n * dispatch layer uses it for the per-task input and output buffers\n * so those allocations participate in the same refcount-driven\n * recycling as the slot cells and the large-value payloads — one\n * heap for every byte that carries a value through the run. May\n * return NULL only if the store itself is NULL. */\nstruct ua    *slot_store_allocator(slot_store_t *s);\n/* }}} */\n\n#ifdef __cplusplus\n}\n#endif\n\n#endif /* SORAMECH_SLOT_STORE_H */\n",

        "content":"1\t/* src/012-dispatch.h — task dispatch layer, public API.\n2\t *\n3\t * What it is, in a sentence: the C function the thread pool runs\n4\t * per task; reads inputs from the slot store, invokes the box's\n5\t * language spec, pushes outputs to downstream input slots, and\n6\t * spawns consumer tasks on input readiness.\n7\t *\n8\t * Designed in issue 304. As of this iteration: real input\n9\t * reading, real spec invocation via per-worker handles, real\n10\t * output pushing with FIFO + 1-cell peek slots, real\n11\t * spawn-on-input-ready chaining under a single-spawn-per-box\n12\t * rule. Iterator multi-spawn, the routing kinds beyond plain,\n13\t * and the large-value heap path are still ahead.\n14\t *\n15\t * The `dispatch_ctx_t` bundle is the durable runtime state for\n16\t * the whole run: graph, slot store, spec registry, pool, plus\n17\t * a per-box \"ever spawned\" flag array and (optionally) a\n18\t * per-box output-capture table for testing. Tasks carry a\n19\t * pointer to the context so the action has everything it needs\n20\t * without globals.\n21\t */\n22\t\n23\t#ifndef SORAMECH_DISPATCH_H\n24\t#define SORAMECH_DISPATCH_H\n25\t\n26\t#include <stdatomic.h>\n27\t#include <stdint.h>\n28\t#include <pthread.h>\n29\t\n30\t#include \"010-graph-loader.h\"\n31\t#include \"011-spec-registry.h\"\n32\t#include \"009-slot-store.h\"\n33\t#include \"014-event-queue.h\"\n34\t#include \"pool.h\"\n35\t\n36\t#ifdef __cplusplus\n37\textern \"C\" {\n38\t#endif\n39\t\n40\t/* {{{ Types */\n41\ttypedef struct dispatch_ctx {\n42\t    const graph_t        *graph;\n43\t    slot_store_t         *slots;\n44\t    spec_registry_t      *specs;\n45\t    pool_t               *pool;\n46\t\n47\t    /* Diagnostic: incremented each time dispatch_action runs. */\n48\t    _Atomic int           tasks_dispatched;\n49\t\n50\t    /* Per-box runtime state (spawn guard + optional output capture)\n51\t     * lives in chunked-append chunks (see ctx_box_chunk_t in\n52\t     * dispatch.c). The old flat-arrays-with-headroom model imposed\n53\t     * a 4096-box runtime cap that issue 319's create_box could\n54\t     * exceed; chunks remove the cap by growing on demand.\n55\t     *\n56\t     * Access goes through the private ctx_box_slot helper in\n57\t     * dispatch.c; callers don't touch these directly. */\n58\t    void                 *box_chunks_opaque;   /* ctx_box_chunk_t**, hidden type */\n59\t    _Atomic unsigned int  n_box_chunks;\n60\t    pthread_mutex_t       box_chunks_mu;\n61\t    int                   capture_outputs;\n62\t\n63\t    /* Optional default output buffer size for invoke calls. If 0,\n64\t     * defaults to 4096. */\n65\t    int                   default_out_capacity;\n66\t\n67\t    /* Optional run-log event queue (issue 311). When non-NULL, the\n68\t     * dispatch action emits task_start + task_end events through\n69\t     * it. NULL turns the per-task logging off; the runner emits\n70\t     * run_start / run_end on its own. */\n71\t    event_queue_t        *events;\n72\t\n73\t    /* Verbose-event gate (issue 311 SORAMECH_LOG_VALUES=1). When\n74\t     * 1 and `events` is non-NULL, dispatch_action also emits\n75\t     * task_input / task_output events with the byte payloads\n76\t     * (truncated to 4 KB per the architecture doc). */\n77\t    int                   log_values;\n78\t\n79\t    /* Slot-event gate (issue 311 SORAMECH_LOG_SLOTS=1).
